It’s way more than I’d ever normally put on a plate for supper, and I’m not really eating because I’m hungry at that point.\n\nDoctor: Does this eating behavior cause you significant distress or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it does. I’m still getting to work and handling my basic responsibilities, but it’s affecting my life. I’ve been turning down plans, like going to the theater or walking with my club, because I feel ashamed and I get stuck thinking about whether I look disciplined enough. It’s… it’s embarrassing, and it takes up a lot of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes usually happen after you've already gone to bed and woken up during the night, or are you still up and about when they start?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I haven’t really paid close attention to that difference. I just know it tends to happen at night, but whether it’s after I’ve gone to bed and woken up or while I’m still up… I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not in control during these episodes, even if you're not physically hungry? Like you can't stop eating even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s honestly what brings me in. Once I start eating at night, it feels like I’m watching myself do it and telling myself, “Stop, that’s enough,” but my hands just keep going back for more. I don’t feel in control of it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes usually happen after you’ve woken up in the middle of the night, or are they more likely to start while you’re still up and about in the evening?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know it’s usually at night, but I haven’t really sorted out whether I had already gone to sleep and woke up, or if I was just still awake in the evening. I’d probably need to pay closer attention to that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I’m still getting to work and handling my basic responsibilities, but it’s affecting my life. It’s… it’s embarrassing, and it takes up a lot of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes usually happen after you've already gone to bed and woken up during the night, or are you still up and about when they start?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I haven’t really paid close attention to that difference. I just know it tends to happen at night, but whether it’s after I’ve gone to bed and woken up or while I’m still up… I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not in control during these episodes, even if you're not physically hungry? Some evenings I’ll start with just leftovers, and then it turns into sandwiches, cereal, cookies, whatever snacks are in the pantry. It’s way more than I’d ever normally put on a plate for supper, and I’m not really eating because I’m hungry at that point.\n\nDoctor: Does this eating behavior cause you significant distress or interfere with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it does. I’m still getting to work and handling my basic responsibilities, but it’s affecting my life. I’ve been turning down plans, like going to the theater or walking with my club, because I feel ashamed and I get stuck thinking about whether I look disciplined enough. It’s… it’s embarrassing, and it takes up a lot of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes usually happen after you've already gone to bed and woken up during the night, or are you still up and about when they start?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I haven’t really paid close attention to that difference. I just know it tends to happen at night, but whether it’s after I’ve gone to bed and woken up or while I’m still up… I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not in control during these episodes, even if you're not physically hungry? Like you can't stop eating even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s honestly what brings me in. Once I start eating at night, it feels like I’m watching myself do it and telling myself, “Stop, that’s enough,” but my hands just keep going back for more. I don’t feel in control of it.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes usually happen after you’ve woken up in the middle of the night, or are they more likely to start while you’re still up and about in the evening?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know it’s usually at night, but I haven’t really sorted out whether I had already gone to sleep and woke up, or if I was just still awake in the evening. I’d probably need to pay closer attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually start eating during these episodes after you've already gone to bed and woken up in the middle of the night?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure. I haven’t really kept track of whether I’d already gone to bed and then woke up, or if I was just still awake when it started. I know it happens at night, but I haven’t paid close enough attention to whether I was already asleep and woke up, or if I just hadn’t gone to bed yet.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if, during these episodes, you feel like you’re eating a much larger amount of food than most people would in a similar period of time, and if it feels like you can’t stop yourself even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes.
